<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="getting-started"><a href="#getting-started" class="header-anchor">#</a> Getting Started</h3> <p>This video by <a href="https://twitter.com/MindExplorers" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Mark Vanstone<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> is a great help to get started.</p> <iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/43mwbL5kp9w?si=USEgTf0fX48NdWmN" title="YouTube video player" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" allowfullscreen="allowfullscreen"></iframe> <h3 id="installation"><a href="#installation" class="header-anchor">#</a> Installation</h3> <h3 id="download-rogue-engine"><a href="#download-rogue-engine" class="header-anchor">#</a> Download Rogue Engine</h3> <p>Now it's time to <a href="https://rogueengine.io/download" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">download Rogue Engine<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a>. Choose the option for your Operating System and the installer will start downloading.</p> <h3 id="install"><a href="#install" class="header-anchor">#</a> Install</h3> <p>On <strong>OSX</strong> the installation should be as simple as any other. Open the installer and drag the Rogue Engine icon into the Applications folder as instructed. If you're using a Silicon Mac (M1, M2, etc) Make sure you're using the arm64 installer.</p> <p>On <strong>Windows</strong> and Debian-based <strong>Linux</strong>, open the installer and follow the instructions. In the case of Windows it may say the app is unrecognized, that's totally normal since the app is not signed. Just hit Run Anyway.</p> <p>If you're using <strong>Arch</strong> btw, or any Arch-based distro, download the .pacman installer and run the following command:</p> <div class="language- extra-class"><pre class="language-text"><code>pacman -U filename.pacman
</code></pre></div><p>The AppImage is no Longer recommended. Use it only if you can't use the .deb or .pacman.</p> <p>You should be able to open Rogue Engine now. Follow me to the next article where I'll show you how to create your first project.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="your-first-project"><a href="#your-first-project" class="header-anchor">#</a> Your First Project</h3> <p>The first thing you'll see when starting Rogue Engine is the start window, where you can create and open projects.</p> <h3 id="creating-a-project"><a href="#creating-a-project" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating a project</h3> <p>To create a project, use the <strong>name</strong> field to give it a name and then select the path where it'll be created. The default path for your projects is <code>documents/RogueProjects</code>.</p> <p>Your project will be created at <code>selected/path/ProjectName</code>, so you don't have to worry about creating a sub-directory with the project name.</p> <p>When you're ready, click on <strong>create</strong>. This will create your project files and install the required node modules so <strong>make sure you are connected to the internet before you do this</strong>.</p> <p>When your project is created it will open in the editor. Now you can start <a href="/Workflow/ImportingAssets">importing assets</a> and <a href="/assets/Scenes">creating Scenes</a> and <a href="/assets/Components">components</a>.</p> <p>Keep in mind that your changes to the default scene will be lost unless you save them!</p> <h3 id="save-your-scene"><a href="#save-your-scene" class="header-anchor">#</a> Save Your Scene</h3> <p>Save your scene with <code>ctrl/cmd + S</code> or select Save Scene in the Rogue Engine Menu. You'll be prompted to provide a name. This will create a Scene file in your currently selected folder.</p> <h3 id="creating-a-scene"><a href="#creating-a-scene" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating a Scene</h3> <p>Select New Scene from the Rogue Engine menu. This will create a fresh scene for you. You can also create a new Scene file from Assets -&gt; Scene.</p> <h3 id="opening-a-project"><a href="#opening-a-project" class="header-anchor">#</a> Opening a project</h3> <p>Next time you start Rogue Engine, you will find the projects previously opened/created, on a list where you can open them with just a click.</p> <p>You can also open your project by clicking on the <strong>Open Project</strong> button.</p> <p>A window will come up where you can navigate to the directory where your project is located. When you've found it, press open and your project will be right where you left it.</p> <p>It if's a recent project, you can go to the Rogue Engine top menu, select Recent and you'll get a list of the last few projects.</p> <h3 id="project-files"><a href="#project-files" class="header-anchor">#</a> Project files</h3> <p>If you navigate to the project folder you'll see that a few files and folders have been created. These are part of the <a href="https://nodejs.org/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Node.js<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> environment of the project and you shouldn't mess with them unless you really know what you're doing.</p> <p>If you do understand what these files are and what they are doing then, lucky you! You have access to npm, the typescript configuration, the webpack configuration among other things there so go nuts, just make sure you don't touch the build process and webpack's current configuration when you're adding stuff.</p> <p>If you break anything just create a new project and compare the configurations to see where you messed up.</p> <h3 id="the-assets-folder"><a href="#the-assets-folder" class="header-anchor">#</a> The Assets folder</h3> <p>The Assets folder is where you should add your 3d models, images, etc. Rogue Engine knows nothing above that folder so make sure you keep your assets in there.</p> <p>You're free to create as many sub-directories as you like and use the project structure that suits you best.</p> <p>Follow me to the next article to explore what you can do next.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="next-steps"><a href="#next-steps" class="header-anchor">#</a> Next Steps</h3> <p>Now that you have Rogue Engine installed and know how to open and create projects there are a few things you can do:</p> <h3 id="watch-tutorials"><a href="#watch-tutorials" class="header-anchor">#</a> Watch tutorials</h3> <p>Watch the video tutorials and other useful content on <a href="https://www.youtube.com/channel/UCPQzvVxStUywZUsLcEuE16g" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">BeardScript's YouTube channel<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a>. You can also benefit from any Three.js tutorial you can find.</p> <iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/OIorPLKWCv4?si=GaSJxNMl_wW2_wR9" title="YouTube video player2" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen="allowfullscreen"></iframe> <iframe width="560" height="315" src="https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/PMmWT3Iuf5g?si=X3qHCvM1GDAHG89A" title="YouTube video player" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" allowfullscreen="allowfullscreen"></iframe> <h3 id="keep-reading-this-guide"><a href="#keep-reading-this-guide" class="header-anchor">#</a> Keep reading this guide</h3> <p>If you're not sure what to do next you should read the <a href="/Workflow/EditorLayout">Editor's Workflow</a> manual. This comprehensive guide will help you understand how the Editor works.</p> <p>The <a href="/EngineAPI/App">Engine API</a> manual is a great place to learn how to code your scripts and components.</p> <h3 id="download-an-example"><a href="#download-an-example" class="header-anchor">#</a> Download an Example</h3> <p>One great thing you can do is checkout the <a href="https://rogueengine.io/examples" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">examples<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> to see how a working project is made. That's a fantastic complement to this guide. Study the components, the project structure, the settings and take your new found knowledge into your projects.</p> <h3 id="learn-three-js"><a href="#learn-three-js" class="header-anchor">#</a> Learn Three.js</h3> <p>Three.js is the WebGL library behind Rogue Engine. It's crucial that you learn how to use it if you're planning to write any code.</p> <p>In the <a href="https://rogueengine.io/#resources" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Resources<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> section of this website you'll find a few guides and books to help you get started.</p> <h3 id="learn-javascript"><a href="#learn-javascript" class="header-anchor">#</a> Learn Javascript</h3> <p>Well... this goes without saying. If you're planning to write any code you'll need to learn Javascript.</p> <p>In the <a href="https://rogueengine.io/#resources" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Resources<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> section of the website you'll find some resources to aid you in just that.</p> <h3 id="learn-typescript"><a href="#learn-typescript" class="header-anchor">#</a> Learn Typescript</h3> <p>If you don't know Javascript, it might be best to learn the basics first. But if you do, you should learn Typescript next. This Rogue Engine's officially supported language.</p> <p>It should be easy enough for a Javascript developer to learn. It might seem intimidating at first but, as you get used to it you'll start seeing the benefits.</p> <p>Most of us hate it at the beginning and end up loving it 😍.</p> <h3 id="join-the-community"><a href="#join-the-community" class="header-anchor">#</a> Join the community</h3> <p>If you haven't yet, <a href="https://rogueengine.io/#community" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">join the community<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a>. This is a great way to interact and learn with other people using either Rogue Engine and Three.js.</p> <p>Be respectful, be mindful and don't hesitate to offer your help if you can. I'm sure someone will reciprocate. It goes both ways.</p> <p><a href="https://x.com/BeardScript" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">BeardScript<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a>🧉 is always around on <a href="https://discord.gg/PnxAsck" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Discord<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> to provide assistance.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="editor-layout"><a href="#editor-layout" class="header-anchor">#</a> Editor Layout</h3> <p>The Editor is divided in two main sections. At the top we have the Toolbar, and right below the Editor Windows section.</p> <h3 id="the-toolbar"><a href="#the-toolbar" class="header-anchor">#</a> The Toolbar</h3> <p>At the left of the toolbar you'll find the transform controls. These allow us set the type of control we wish to use to mutate the transform properties of an <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/core/Object3D" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Object3D<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> present in the current <a href="/assets/Scenes">Scene</a>.</p> <p>At the center of the toolbar you'll find the runtime controls. These are the play and pause button. When you press play, the Scene will start running and instead of the play button you'll see a stop one.</p> <h3 id="editor-windows"><a href="#editor-windows" class="header-anchor">#</a> Editor Windows</h3> <p>The editor windows provide useful visualization and controls for different aspects of the editing process.</p> <p>Let's look at each of the default Editor Windows one by one:</p> <h4 id="project-window"><a href="#project-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Project Window</h4> <p>The Project Window contains a file manager where you have access to all of your assets. Feel free to organize them however you want.</p> <h4 id="console-window"><a href="#console-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Console Window</h4> <p>The Console Window is where you'll see any logs coming from the <a href="/EngineAPI/Debug">Debug</a> class.</p> <h4 id="hierarchy-window"><a href="#hierarchy-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Hierarchy Window</h4> <p>The Hierarchy provides a Hierarchical representation of the object in the loaded Scene. It allows you to select and nest objects at will. By default it will be deactivated when playing your scenes but, you can change this by unchecking the <strong>Diable on Play</strong> box at the top.</p> <h4 id="scene-window"><a href="#scene-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Scene Window</h4> <p>The Scene Window is where you can see and visually edit your loaded Scene. When pressing play on the Toolbar all editing controls will be deactivated and you'll be able to interact as your user would.</p> <h4 id="inspector-window"><a href="#inspector-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Inspector Window</h4> <p>The Inspector shows us all the useful controls to monitor and modify the properties and components of objects in the scene.</p> <h4 id="skybox-window"><a href="#skybox-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Skybox Window</h4> <p>The Skybox Window lets set and modify our Scene's skybox.
You have two different modes to select: procedural and cubemap. If you feel this feature is hurting the performance of your app or, it's simply irrelevant, you can disable it by unchecking the <strong>enabled</strong> box.</p> <h4 id="asset-manager-window"><a href="#asset-manager-window" class="header-anchor">#</a> Asset Manager Window</h4> <p>The Asset Manager lets us set our Asset's loading configurations for the current Scene. See the <a href="/Workflow/AssetManager">Asset Manager</a> section for more info.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="visual-components"><a href="#visual-components" class="header-anchor">#</a> Visual Components</h3> <p>Visual Components are Rogue Engine Components that can be composed visually in the VisualComponent Editor.</p> <h3 id="creating-a-visual-component"><a href="#creating-a-visual-component" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating a Visual Component</h3> <p>To create a Visual Component, right click on a folder in your project and select <code>New Visual Component</code>.</p> <h3 id="editing-a-visual-component"><a href="#editing-a-visual-component" class="header-anchor">#</a> Editing a Visual Component</h3> <p>To edit a visual component, double click it within the editor and the VisualComponent Editor will open with the selected component tab. If you just created it, wait for the editor to finish bundling, you'll see the play button turn into a spinner and then switch back in a few seconds when it's ready.</p> <p>Press <code>ctrl/cmd + P</code> to open the VisualComponent menu, where you can search and select one to edit.</p> <p>When you make changes you'll see a floppy disk icon appear in the tab. Press the icon or use <code>ctrl/cmd + S</code> to save. If you close without saving, your changes will be lost.</p> <p>To close a specific component editor simply click the <code>x</code> icon on the tab.</p> <h3 id="layout"><a href="#layout" class="header-anchor">#</a> Layout</h3> <p>When you open a blank component the only thing you'll see the bare-bones skeleton layout.</p> <p>First you'll see the Fields section where you'll be able to define and configure the component's fields.</p> <p>Then starts the method folders. These will execute their contents at sone specific times. These ara analogous to their equivalent component methods that you can find in the code.</p> <p>Let's see them one by one:</p> <ul><li>Awake: runs when the component is first enabled or fired up. No internal resources or assets are available at this point.</li> <li>Start: runs when the component is ready to run, right before the game-loop begins.</li> <li>Update: runs every frame, basically, this is the game loop itself.</li></ul> <p>At the bottom, there's a button called <code>Add Method</code> to well... add methods. More on Methods later in the document.</p> <h3 id="fields"><a href="#fields" class="header-anchor">#</a> Fields</h3> <p>Fields are there to help us store, manipulate and visualize data in our components. Everything from the speed of a vehicle, to the jump force of a character.</p> <p>To add a field, simply click on the plus icon.</p> <p>You should select a type of data and click on the name to rename it. Press enter to save it.</p> <p>To delete a field, press the <code>x</code> icon to its right.</p> <p>While selecting a type alone is enough most of the time, <code>Array</code> and <code>Component</code> types require an additional <code>valueType</code>. In the case of Arrays, this tells the engine what type of values does the array store. In the case of Components we can specify what type of component it is.</p> <p>The search menu will provide you with the components available in your project so if you select a component type directly, it'll automatically set the type to Component and the valueType to the selected component type.</p> <p>Most fields have two modes: <code>regular</code> and <code>inspect</code>. Component fields additionally, can be <code>require</code>.</p> <p><code>regular</code>: just a regular field
<code>inspect</code>: Show it in the inspector. It may also provide you with other configuration options for the visual interface depending on the component type. For example, setting both the min/max values in a Number type field will display a slider in the inspector.
<code>require</code>: it sets the field to a component of the same type, present in the same object. Additionally, you can set a specific component name to look for and/or whether we want to look for the component upwards in the hierarchy as well, to for example, get it from a parent.</p> <h4 id="using-fields"><a href="#using-fields" class="header-anchor">#</a> Using Fields</h4> <p>You can set the value of a field in your component using the <code>SetField</code> Brick. Additionally, you can get or set the value of a field in another Visual Component using the <code>VC:GetField</code> and <code>VC:SetField</code> Bricks.</p> <h3 id="bricks"><a href="#bricks" class="header-anchor">#</a> Bricks</h3> <p>Bricks are encapsuled units of functionality that you can add to your component. Generally they're so simple in nature that you can safely ask the Rogue AI to create them an trust that the result will be very unlikely to fail. Ultimately, you own your solution by stacking your bricks in a way that makes sense for your project, regardless of who wrote the code behind them.</p> <p><strong>Tip</strong>
You can navigate through your components bricks either by scrolling or with the <code>up</code> and <code>down</code> arrows.</p> <p>There are four types of Bricks.</p> <ul><li><strong>Actions:</strong> synchronous actions that you can trust to run sequentially, like moving objects, setting values, etc.</li> <li><strong>Blocks:</strong> these are actions that provide parameters to a block of actions that run in a specific way. Two examples of these are <code>Repeat</code> and <code>Iterate</code>.</li> <li><strong>Events:</strong> these are actions that provide parameters to a block of actions that will run at some point when certain conditions are met. This is used for collision detection and api calls, as an example.</li> <li><strong>Conditions:</strong> this is a special type of brick used for the <code>IF</code> brick. It runs a block of actions synchronously when a condition is met. The <code>IF</code> also supports <code>else</code> and <code>else if</code> which you can easily add/remove by right clicking the brick itself.</li></ul> <h3 id="slots"><a href="#slots" class="header-anchor">#</a> Slots</h3> <p>Bricks can provide a set of parameters, also known as Slots. These allow us to configure the behavior of a brick. For example, a brick might let us set which object we want to apply movement to. We can set a slot to take the value of a field, an action a variable, an expression, or a primitive value (numbers, strings, booleans).</p> <p>Sometimes a slot will offer you specific values pre-set by the developer.</p> <p><strong>Tip</strong>
You can navigate through slots with the <code>left</code> and <code>right</code> arrows of the selected brick.</p> <h3 id="adding-bricks"><a href="#adding-bricks" class="header-anchor">#</a> Adding Bricks</h3> <p>There are three ways to add bricks:</p> <ul><li><strong>Add Button:</strong> when you open one of the method folders you'll find an <code>Add</code> button you can press to open the search menu.</li> <li><strong>Right Click:</strong> right click on a brick and select <code>Add Before</code> or <code>Add After</code>.</li> <li><strong>Keyboard:</strong> with a brick selected press <code>shift + A</code> to add after or <code>ctrl/cdm + shift + A</code> to add before.</li></ul> <p>A search menu will open where you'll be able to select a brick. You can search by its name or category. In the future we'll support search tags as well.</p> <h3 id="removing-bricks"><a href="#removing-bricks" class="header-anchor">#</a> Removing Bricks</h3> <p>To remove a brick you can:</p> <ul><li><strong>Right Click:</strong> right click on a brick and select <code>Remove</code>.</li> <li><strong>Keyboard:</strong> with a brick selected press the <code>Delete</code> key.</li></ul> <h3 id="setting-slots"><a href="#setting-slots" class="header-anchor">#</a> Setting Slots</h3> <p>To set a slot value you can left click, or if you're navigating with the keyboard, press <code>Enter</code> on the slot.</p> <p>The search menu will open and you'll be offered only fields, actions, variables, expressions and primitive values that are compatible with the type of that slot.</p> <h3 id="expressions"><a href="#expressions" class="header-anchor">#</a> Expressions</h3> <p>These provide an easy way to express from the simplest to the most complex numeric or boolean expressions. You can easily identify them by their dashed border. You'll also get relevant operators and elements to use in your expressions, including parenthesis.</p> <p>You can add elements to your expression in multiple ways:</p> <ul><li><strong>Right Click:</strong> right click on an element of the expression and select <code>Add Before</code> or <code>Add After</code>.</li> <li><strong>Keyboard:</strong> with the expression selected press <code>space</code> to add after or <code>shift + space</code> to add before.</li></ul> <p>To remove an element right click and select <code>Remove</code> or when navigating with the keyboard press the <code>Delete</code> key.</p> <h3 id="variables"><a href="#variables" class="header-anchor">#</a> Variables</h3> <p>Every Action Brick that returns one or more values will present them to you as variables that you can use in compatible slots of other Bricks.</p> <p>You can give these variables a friendly name by right-clicking on them and selecting <code>rename</code>.</p> <p>You can also set a variable's value using the special Action brick <code>SetVar</code>.</p> <p>Additionally, there's a super useful brick called <code>Var</code> which you'll find in the Brick search menu.</p> <p>Behind the scene this is simply an Action Brick disguised as contextual data storage... a variable.</p> <p>You can set any sort of value or expression in a <code>Var</code> and have the result stored with a friendly name, making it easier to read and manage your component's behavior.</p> <h3 id="methods"><a href="#methods" class="header-anchor">#</a> Methods</h3> <p>Methods are self-contained sets of Bricks that you can execute with the <code>RunMethod</code> Brick. Additionally you can run Methods in other Visual Components using the <code>VC:RunMethod</code> Brick.</p> <p>When are they useful?</p> <ul><li>Organizing logical chunks of functionality in your component.</li> <li>Chunks of functionality that needs to be used in multiple places.</li> <li>Chunks of functionality that can to be used by other components.</li></ul> <h3 id="block-parameters"><a href="#block-parameters" class="header-anchor">#</a> Block Parameters</h3> <p>These are contextual variables provided to <code>Event</code> and <code>Block</code> Bricks as well as <code>Methods</code>.</p> <p>In the case of <code>Event</code> and <code>Block</code> Bricks you'll find them at the top of the block. Same as with any variable, they are prefixed with the alphanumeric id of the action.</p> <p>In the case of <code>Methods</code> they're the local parameters, so it's only relevant if you're creating it. In this case, they're prefixed with the name of the method instead of an id.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="importing-assets"><a href="#importing-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Importing Assets</h3> <p>Importing external Assets is a crucial part of the development experience in Rogue Engine or any engine. You need a way to get that cute Pikachu model you've been working on, into the editor</p> <h3 id="how-to-import-external-assets"><a href="#how-to-import-external-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> How to import external Assets</h3> <p>There's two ways to go about this:</p> <h4 id="_1-os-file-manager"><a href="#_1-os-file-manager" class="header-anchor">#</a> 1. OS file manager</h4> <p>You can just go into your Assets folder with your OS file manager and paste your files there. The editor will automatically refresh and display the new files in the <strong>Project</strong> window.</p> <h4 id="_2-import-assets-menu"><a href="#_2-import-assets-menu" class="header-anchor">#</a> 2. Import Assets menu</h4> <p>Right click on the target folder and select the <strong>Import Assets</strong> option in the context menu. A dialog will come up for you to choose your files.</p> <h4 id="bonus-terminal"><a href="#bonus-terminal" class="header-anchor">#</a> Bonus: Terminal</h4> <p>Yeah that, use your terminal you beautiful little nerd ❤.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="loading-assets"><a href="#loading-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Loading Assets</h3> <p>There are two types of Assets that you might want to load: Rogue Assets, and external Assets.</p> <h3 id="rogue-assets"><a href="#rogue-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Rogue Assets</h3> <p>These are <a href="/assets/Scenes">Scenes</a>, <a href="/assets/Materials">Materials</a>, <a href="/assets/AudioAssets">Audio Assets</a>, <a href="/assets/Components">Components</a>, and <a href="/assets/Prefabs">Prefabs</a>. To learn how to "load" any of these check their respective articles.</p> <h3 id="external-assets"><a href="#external-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> External Assets</h3> <p>These can be Images, 3d models, audio files, etc. There are multiple ways you can use them depending on the type of file. Let's look at some of them.</p> <h4 id="images"><a href="#images" class="header-anchor">#</a> Images</h4> <p>Images can be used by Materials and the Skybox. To load them, drop them on an Image field in the Inspector.</p> <p><strong>Supported image formats: jpg, png.</strong></p> <h4 id="_3d-models"><a href="#_3d-models" class="header-anchor">#</a> 3d Models</h4> <p>To load a 3D model you can drag the file and drop it either directly on the <strong>Scene</strong> window, the <strong>Hierarchy</strong> or a particular object in the <strong>Hierarchy</strong> if you wish to nest it.</p> <p><strong>Supported 3d model formats: gltf, fbx, obj.</strong></p> <p><strong>Note:</strong> Please use <strong>GLTF</strong> whenever possible. This is the better supported format for WebGL.</p> <h4 id="audio-files"><a href="#audio-files" class="header-anchor">#</a> Audio Files</h4> <p>Audio Files can be turned into <a href="/assets/AudioAssets">Audio Assets</a> that you can use in your <a href="/assets/Components">Components</a>. Right click on your file and select the <strong>Create Audio Asset</strong> option from the context menu.</p> <p><strong>Supported audio formats: wav, mp3, ogg, mkv.</strong></p> <h3 id="static-assets"><a href="#static-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Static Assets</h3> <p>Another way to load assets is to treat them as static files. To do this you just have to put them inside <em>ProjectFolder/Static</em>. This route will be available from your built project as well so, go nuts... but not too much eh! Remember the loading times and all that. Fetch them wisely.</p> <h3 id="named-prefabs"><a href="#named-prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Named Prefabs</h3> <p>Probably, the most powerful feature in Rogue Engine after the components are <strong>Prefabs</strong>. We can load them by dropping them in a prefab field in the inspector or, alternatively, we can use Named Prefabs.</p> <p>Named Prefabs are all the prefab files located under <code>Assets/Prefabs/</code>. These can be dynamically loaded without the need to reference them in a component.</p> <p>We can do this using <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab#static-instantiate">RE.Prefab.instantiate()</a>, <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab#static-fetch">RE.Prefab.fetch()</a>, <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab#static-get">RE.Prefab.get()</a>.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="asset-manager"><a href="#asset-manager" class="header-anchor">#</a> Asset Manager</h3> <p>The Asset Manager is in charge of loading and keeping track of the Assets being referenced by our components and their configuration.</p> <p>The Asset Manager window is located at the right of the editor. You'll find the tab stacked with the <strong>Skybox</strong> and the <strong>Inspector</strong>.</p> <h3 id="how-does-it-work"><a href="#how-does-it-work" class="header-anchor">#</a> How does it work?</h3> <p>Each Scene has its own Asset Configurations and when you open a Scene in the editor the Asset Manager will set its own configurations.</p> <p>At runtime, when a Scene is being loaded, it sends the Asset Manager its Asset Configurations. This will wipe out any remaining Assets that are not set to <strong>keep loaded</strong> and set the new configurations.</p> <p>When that's done, the preloading process will begin and the scene will not finish loading until it's over.</p> <h3 id="asset-configurations"><a href="#asset-configurations" class="header-anchor">#</a> Asset Configurations</h3> <p>On the Asset Manager window, you'll find a list of all <strong>Prefabs</strong>, <strong>Materials</strong> and <strong>AudioAssets</strong> in your project. These are the only types of assets that we can reference in our components and thus the only ones to which we can apply a configuration.</p> <p>You can set An Asset to <strong>preload</strong>, <strong>keep loaded</strong>, and/or <strong>override</strong>.</p> <h4 id="preload"><a href="#preload" class="header-anchor">#</a> Preload</h4> <p>Setting an asset to <strong>preload</strong> tells the <a href="/assets/Scenes">Scene</a> that we want to fetch and load that asset before the Scene is initialized.</p> <p>This is great for things that need to be readily available. Keep in mind that the more the assets you set to preload the longer it will take for your Scene to start.</p> <h4 id="keep-loaded"><a href="#keep-loaded" class="header-anchor">#</a> Keep Loaded</h4> <p>This option tells the <a href="/assets/Scenes">Scene</a> that we want this Asset to stay loaded in the next Scene. This is a neat way to keep your loading time short when you know an Asset will be used in other scenes.</p> <h4 id="override"><a href="#override" class="header-anchor">#</a> override</h4> <p>When this setting is set to true, the current configuration will be carried over all the following scenes, overriding any other configuration for the given Asset. This is very handy mostly when you wish to keep an asset loaded perpetually without the need to set its <strong>keep loaded</strong> property on every scene.</p> <p>If an asset configuration for the incoming scene has an <strong>override</strong> this will take preference over an old one, thus, stopping its perpetuation.</p> <h3 id="saving-configurations"><a href="#saving-configurations" class="header-anchor">#</a> Saving Configurations</h3> <p>Asset Configurations are saved in the Scene so, whenever you make a change in the Asset Manager window, remember to save your scene.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="building-your-project"><a href="#building-your-project" class="header-anchor">#</a> Building Your Project</h3> <p>In order to share your project with the world, you'll need to build it.</p> <p>Rogue Engine generates a ready-to-deploy web build that you can serve through a web server.</p> <h3 id="instructions"><a href="#instructions" class="header-anchor">#</a> Instructions</h3> <p>To build your project go to <em>File -&gt; Project Settings</em>. A dialog will pop up where you can set the name of your project, the scenes you wish to load and the <strong>build</strong> button.</p> <p>Set a name for your project and drop the scene files in the <strong>Scenes</strong> list. The first Scene on the list will be your project's initial Scene.</p> <p>When you're ready press <strong>build</strong> and wait until the build process is complete. When that happens, the folder with your built project will open automatically.</p> <p>Your built project will be saved at the root of your project with the name <strong>Build_[timestamp]</strong>.</p> <h3 id="built-project-files"><a href="#built-project-files" class="header-anchor">#</a> Built project files</h3> <p>If you look into your built project you'll find an HTML file, the Data folder and the Assets folder.</p> <h4 id="data-folder"><a href="#data-folder" class="header-anchor">#</a> Data folder</h4> <p>This folder has the name of your project with "_Data" at the end. It contains all the built assets and the bundled javascript.</p> <h4 id="assets-folder"><a href="#assets-folder" class="header-anchor">#</a> Assets folder</h4> <p>If your project has any <a href="/Workflow/StaticAssets">static assets</a> the Assets folder will have your <strong>static</strong> folder with all it's contents.</p> <h4 id="index-html"><a href="#index-html" class="header-anchor">#</a> index.html</h4> <p>This is the file with the execution code for your project.</p> <h3 id="running-a-built-project"><a href="#running-a-built-project" class="header-anchor">#</a> Running a built project</h3> <p>If you open the <strong>index.html</strong> directly your project will not work bacause it needs to be served through a web server. This can be something like the Live Server extension of VSCode to run it locally or a proper online web server.</p> <p>The smoothest and most stable browser to run Three.js projects at the moment is Microsoft Edge.</p> <p>Keep in mind that Firefox has some performance issues with WebGL.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="static-assets"><a href="#static-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Static Assets</h3> <p>In Rogue Engine there's an established pipeline to make it simple for you to use your static assets. Don't worry though, remember that this is all built around a familiar web environment, so it's nothing any web developer hasn't seen before.</p> <h3 id="the-static-folder"><a href="#the-static-folder" class="header-anchor">#</a> The Static Folder</h3> <p>The first and most important thing is that you store your static assets in <em>ProjectFolder/Static/</em>. This folder will be accessible both in development and in your built project.</p> <h3 id="static-folder-url"><a href="#static-folder-url" class="header-anchor">#</a> Static folder url</h3> <p>The url of the static folder will not be the same in development and in your built project. In order to get the correct path from your code you should use the <a href="/EngineAPI/Functions#getstaticpath">getStaticPath</a> function, which will attach the static folder address to a given path.</p> <h3 id="built-project"><a href="#built-project" class="header-anchor">#</a> Built Project</h3> <p>If you have any static files, your built project will include the <em>/Static/</em> folder with all its contents.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="marketplace"><a href="#marketplace" class="header-anchor">#</a> Marketplace</h3> <p>The Marketplace is a great place to find assets, components, editor extensions and other utilities to help you with your project. These are distributed in the form of packages that you can install directly on your project.</p> <h3 id="accessing-the-marketplace"><a href="#accessing-the-marketplace" class="header-anchor">#</a> Accessing the Marketplace</h3> <p>You can find the marketplace in the "store" icon in the upper right corner of the editor. Alternatively you can open it with <code>alt/opt + m</code>. You must have logged in from the editor home screen to use the Marketplace.</p> <h3 id="installing-packages"><a href="#installing-packages" class="header-anchor">#</a> Installing Packages</h3> <p>To install a package we need to have the Marketplace opened within the editor and make sure that we're logged in.</p> <p>Now search the asset that you wish to install and simply hit install or and open it. You'll find a find the <code>Install</code> button near the title in the package's page as well. The package will be downloaded and then installed within your project at: <code>rogue-packages/publisher/package-id</code></p> <h3 id="updating-packages"><a href="#updating-packages" class="header-anchor">#</a> Updating Packages</h3> <p>To update a package follow the same steps to install it. When installing it the old files in <code>rogue-packages/publisher/package-id</code> will be replaced with the new ones.</p> <h3 id="creating-packages"><a href="#creating-packages" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating Packages</h3> <p>To create a package, you must hold a seat for a <a href="">paid Rogue Engine license</a>.</p> <p>Make sure that all your package files are in <code>rogue-packages/username/package-id</code>. The <code>username</code> can be yours or the username of an account where you hold a seat with <code>write permission</code> for packages.</p> <p>Go into the marketplace and select <code>+ create New</code> and fill up the form.</p> <p>Make sure the <code>Owner</code> field coincides with the <code>username</code> in the package's path as shown above.</p> <p>Keep in mind that <code>public</code> visibility will not take effect until you <a href="#publishing-packages">submit your package for publishing</a>.</p> <p><strong>The identifier</strong> that you input, must coincide with the <code>package-id</code> in the path as shown above.</p> <p>If the code in your package requires <code>npm dependencies</code> to be installed, make sure you add them in the corresponding field.</p> <p>If your package makes use of other packages in the marketplace, make sure you include them as <code>publisher/package-id</code> each separated by a space.</p> <p><em>Dependencies will be installed before your own package</em></p> <p>When you're ready, hit <code>Submit</code> and wait for the package to be uploaded. It may take a minute so it's a good chance to go get a refill.</p> <p>Now you'll be able to find your package in the <code>My Packages</code> tab of the Marketplace window.</p> <h4 id="updating-your-package"><a href="#updating-your-package" class="header-anchor">#</a> Updating Your Package</h4> <p>To update your own package, first search for it in the marketplace. You'll see an <code>update</code> button right beside it - select that.</p> <p>Now same as when you created it, fill up the form. If you're only updating its information, leave the <code>Update Package Content</code> box unchecked. If you're actually updating any file in the package's contents, make sure that you check that box and that the updated contents are indeed available in <code>rogue-packages/username/package-id</code>.</p> <h3 id="publishing-packages"><a href="#publishing-packages" class="header-anchor">#</a> Publishing Packages</h3> <p>To publish a package of your own creation, first make sure that its visibility is set to <code>public</code> and then submit your request to <code>support@rogueengine.io</code>. At the moment, you can only publish free packages.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="input-manager"><a href="#input-manager" class="header-anchor">#</a> Input Manager</h3> <p>The Input Manager Window provides a visual interface to view and edit your project's input configuration.</p> <p>It provides an action-based system to help you easily bind, rebind and set multiple mappings for different input devices.</p> <p>Check out the Input section of these docs to see the API.</p> <p>To open the Input Manager Window click on <code>Window -&gt; Input Manager</code> or press <code>alt/opt + I</code>.</p> <h3 id="input-groups"><a href="#input-groups" class="header-anchor">#</a> Input Groups</h3> <p>The Rogue Engine Input system provides Input Groups to define multiple mappings for different purposes.</p> <p>As an example, you could have a mapping to control a character with gamepad or mouse and keyboard, and a second mapping to drive for when your character jumps into a car. We do this by leveraging Input Groups.</p> <p>The main Input Group you can see in the window is the default. Input Actions under this group can be accessed from your code or Visual Component Bricks by their names alone.</p> <h4 id="adding-groups"><a href="#adding-groups" class="header-anchor">#</a> Adding Groups</h4> <p>To create our own separate mapping we need to create a Group. For this we simply press the Add Input Group at the bottom.</p> <p>Click on the name and rename it to something more familiar, relevant to your project.</p> <p>To access Actions in your Group you'll do it as <code>[GroupName].[ActionName]</code>.</p> <h3 id="input-actions"><a href="#input-actions" class="header-anchor">#</a> Input Actions</h3> <p>Input Actions are user interaction within our project. They are always part of an Input Group, either the default or a custom one.</p> <p>Action can be either Buttons or Axes. In the case of Axes, we can provide an analog or button source, depending on the chosen input device.</p> <h4 id="adding-actions"><a href="#adding-actions" class="header-anchor">#</a> Adding Actions</h4> <p>To add an action press one of the buttons beside an Input Group: <code>Action Button</code> or <code>Action Axes</code>.</p> <p>A blank Action will show up with a list of input devices to add.</p> <p>Give your Input Action a more familiar name by clicking on the current name tag.</p> <h4 id="adding-input-sources"><a href="#adding-input-sources" class="header-anchor">#</a> Adding Input Sources</h4> <p>All Input Actions, will give you a list of Input Sources to choose from. The configuration of each will be quite different if they're a Button or Axes Action.</p> <p>To add an input source simply click on it, then choose your desired keys, buttons or analog axes.</p> <h3 id="saving-configuration"><a href="#saving-configuration" class="header-anchor">#</a> Saving Configuration</h3> <p>Always remember to press the Save button. Your configuration will be stored in the <code>rogue-config.json</code> file at the base folder of your project. You can copy the <code>inputConfig</code> field and paste in on other projects to load the same configuration. Just make sure to reload the project.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="scenes"><a href="#scenes" class="header-anchor">#</a> Scenes</h3> <p>Scenes are the scenario where we display our assets and create our environments. In the case of games, they can be treated as independent levels or even portions of the same map. To give you an example, you could load a whole new scene every time a player goes through a door.</p> <p>Scenes are stored in <strong>.rogueScene</strong> files that contain the serialization of the three.js <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/scenes/Scene" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Scene<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> object, the components being used by the objects in the scene, the <a href="/Workflow/EditorLayout#skybox-window">Skybox</a>, and the <a href="/Workflow/AssetManager#asset-configurations">asset configurations</a>.</p> <h3 id="default-scene"><a href="#default-scene" class="header-anchor">#</a> Default Scene</h3> <p>When opening the editor you'll see the default scene where you can start creating right away. Don't forget to <a href="#saving-a-scene">save it</a>!</p> <h3 id="creating-a-scene"><a href="#creating-a-scene" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating a Scene</h3> <p>To create a scene, go to the <strong>Project window</strong>, right-click on to the folder where you want your file to reside and select <em>Assets -&gt; Scene</em>. You can also do this by first selecting the target folder and then selecting <em>Assets -&gt; Scene</em> on the top menu.</p> <h3 id="saving-a-scene"><a href="#saving-a-scene" class="header-anchor">#</a> Saving a Scene</h3> <p>Don't forget to save your work. Go to <em>File -&gt; Save Scene</em> often and keep your work safe. If the scene we're editing was opened from a file, that's where it'll be saved. If otherwise, the scene has no file, you'll be prompted to set a name and a new file with the given name will be created in the currently selected folder of the <strong>Project Window</strong>.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="materials"><a href="#materials" class="header-anchor">#</a> Materials</h3> <p>Materials are essential for making things look good. They are used by <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/objects/Mesh" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Meshes<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> to provide texture and other graphic sugar.</p> <p>When you select a Mesh in the <strong>Hierarhy</strong> or the <strong>Scene</strong> window you'll see its Material in the <strong>inspector</strong>. Said Material can either be unique to that mesh, or a reference to a Material Asset, stored in a <strong>.rogueMaterial</strong> file.</p> <p>The <strong>.rogueMaterial</strong> file contains the serialization of a Three.js <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/materials/Material" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Material<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> and it can be used by any number of meshes in the same project.</p> <p>The Material will be loaded by the <a href="/Workflow/AssetManager">AssetManager</a> and all Meshes using the same Material will have access to the same instance.</p> <h3 id="saving-a-material"><a href="#saving-a-material" class="header-anchor">#</a> Saving a Material</h3> <p>To save your material to a <strong>.rogueMaterial</strong> file, you'll need to give it a name and click on Save. Your file will be saved in the selected folder of the <strong>Project</strong> window.</p> <h3 id="creating-a-material"><a href="#creating-a-material" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating a Material</h3> <p>To create a Material, right click on the target folder and select <em>Assets -&gt; [TypeOfMaterial]</em></p> <p>Alternatively you can select the target folder and click on <em>Assets -&gt; [TypeOfMaterial]</em> on the top menu.</p> <h3 id="setting-a-material"><a href="#setting-a-material" class="header-anchor">#</a> Setting a Material</h3> <p>To set a Material to a Mesh, simply drag the Material file and drop it on the Material field of that Mesh in the inspector. The old material will be removed and the new one will be set in its place.</p> <h3 id="everything-changes"><a href="#everything-changes" class="header-anchor">#</a> Everything changes</h3> <p>Remember that if you use the same Material on multiple objects, these will have access to the same instance. This means that when you make changes to one of them, it'll be reflected on all the others.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="components"><a href="#components" class="header-anchor">#</a> Components</h3> <p>Components are scripts that you can add to objects in a scene. They make it dead easy to provide functionality and interaction to your project.</p> <p>Just like all the code in your project, Components can be written in either TypeScript or JavaScript.</p> <h3 id="ready-state"><a href="#ready-state" class="header-anchor">#</a> Ready State</h3> <p>Rogue Components are Asynchronous by nature. For this reason they come equipped with a <strong>ready state</strong> feature which lets the engine know that all of the assets being referenced in its inspector interface are loaded.</p> <p>A component will stay idle until it's <strong>ready</strong>.</p> <p>You can tailor your approach by setting the loading configuration of your Assets in the <a href="/Workflow/AssetManager">Asset Manager</a> window.</p> <h3 id="creating-components"><a href="#creating-components" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating Components</h3> <p>There are two ways to create a component. the first is to right click on the target folder and in the Assets menu select either <strong>Component</strong> for a TypeScript component or <strong>Component JS</strong> for a JavaScript component.</p> <p><a href="https://www.typescriptlang.org/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">TypeScript<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> is the officially supported language and the recommended option. If you can, please, use TypeScript ❤.</p> <p>The second way to create a component is to select the target folder and select either <strong>Component</strong> or <strong>Component JS</strong> from the Asset options on the top menu.</p> <p>In both cases you'll be prompted to provide a name for your component. When you accept your component will be created in the target folder.</p> <h3 id="editting-components"><a href="#editting-components" class="header-anchor">#</a> Editting Components</h3> <p>Editing components can be as simple as double clicking them or <em>right click -&gt; open</em> the file. It will open in your favorite code editor but, this not the best solution.</p> <p><strong>The recommended way</strong> of doing this is to open your project folder in your code editor (VSCode, Sublime, etc.) first, and just then opening the component you wish to edit. This will open it in the context of the project.</p> <p>opening a component can be as simple as double clicking it or <em>right click -&gt; open</em> the file.</p> <p>This method works perfectly with <a href="https://code.visualstudio.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">VSCode<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a>, which is the officially recommended editor.</p> <p>If you don't open your project folder in your code editor first, the component file will open in isolation or in whatever current session you might have open. This is far from ideal.</p> <h3 id="adding-components"><a href="#adding-components" class="header-anchor">#</a> Adding Components</h3> <p>To add a component to an object you need to select it. The drag and drop your file on the <strong>Add Component</strong> drop zone in the inspector.</p> <p>Alternatively, click the <strong>Add Component</strong> text in the drop zone and select a component from the list.</p> <h3 id="removing-components"><a href="#removing-components" class="header-anchor">#</a> Removing Components</h3> <p>To remove a component click the cog on the right and select the <strong>Remove</strong> option.</p> <h3 id="component-interface"><a href="#component-interface" class="header-anchor">#</a> Component Interface</h3> <p>The graphic interface of a component is defined within the component's class. You can visualize and modify variables through property controllers. These also allow you to reference objects within the scene, Prefabs, and other Assets.</p> <h3 id="api"><a href="#api" class="header-anchor">#</a> API</h3> <p>For more information on how components work, visit the <a href="/EngineAPI/Component">Component</a> class code reference.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="audio-assets"><a href="#audio-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Audio Assets</h3> <p>Audio Assets provide the means to generate <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/audio/Audio" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Audio<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> objects based on their buffer information.</p> <p>Audio Assets are serialized and stored in <strong>.rogueAudio</strong> files.</p> <h3 id="creating-audio-assets"><a href="#creating-audio-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating Audio Assets</h3> <p>To create an Audio Asset, you need to import your audio file (mp3, wav, ogg, mkv) into your project. Then, right click on that file and select <strong>Create Audio Asset</strong>. This will create a <strong>.rogueAudio</strong> file right beside the original.</p> <h3 id="using-audio-assets"><a href="#using-audio-assets" class="header-anchor">#</a> Using Audio Assets</h3> <p>To use an Audio Asset, drag and drop it on an <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/audio/Audio" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Audio<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> or <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/audio/PositionalAudio" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">PositionalAudio<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> field in the inspector interface of a component.</p> <h3 id="learn-more"><a href="#learn-more" class="header-anchor">#</a> Learn More</h3> <p>To learn more about Audio Assets see the documentation for the <a href="/EngineAPI/AudioAsset">AudioAsset</a> class.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="prefabs"><a href="#prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Prefabs</h3> <p>Prefabs are pre-fabricated objects stored in <strong>.roguePrefab</strong> files that we can drop into our scene or set as a reference of a <strong>Prefab</strong> field in a <a href="/EngineAPI/Component">Component</a>.</p> <h3 id="creating-prefabs"><a href="#creating-prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Creating Prefabs</h3> <p>To create a prefab grab an object in the <strong>Hierarchy</strong> window and drop it on the target folder. A <strong>.roguePrefab</strong> file with the same name of the given object will be created.</p> <h3 id="loading-prefabs"><a href="#loading-prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Loading Prefabs</h3> <p>Loading a prefab is as simple as dragging a <strong>.roguePrefab</strong> file and dropping it on the <strong>Scene</strong> or <strong>Hierarchy</strong> window.</p> <p>You can also instantiate your Prefabs from your components, through code. In order to do this, you first need to drop your <strong>.roguePrefab</strong> file into the <strong>Prefab</strong> field of a component's interface in the inspector.</p> <h3 id="loading-named-prefabs"><a href="#loading-named-prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Loading Named Prefabs</h3> <p>Named Prefabs are all the prefab files located under <code>Assets/Prefabs/</code>. These can be dynamically loaded without the need to reference them in a component.</p> <p>We can do this using <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab#static-instantiate">RE.Prefab.instantiate()</a>, <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab#static-fetch">RE.Prefab.fetch()</a>, <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab#static-get">RE.Prefab.get()</a>.</p> <h3 id="editing-prefabs"><a href="#editing-prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Editing Prefabs</h3> <p>To edit a prefab you can double click on the file or in the Asset Manager window (alt/option + A). This will open the file in the Prefab Editor. This is a dedicated scene where you can edit your prefab in isolation. After performing your changes, press <code>ctrl/cmd + S</code> or click on <code>Save And Exit</code> in the Hierarchy.</p> <p>Another way to edit a prefab is to simply drop it in the scene, make changes and drop it back on any folder in the Project view. It'll always update the original prefab File.</p> <h3 id="duplicating-prefabs"><a href="#duplicating-prefabs" class="header-anchor">#</a> Duplicating Prefabs</h3> <p>To create a new prefab based on the current one, open it in the Prefab Editor or drop it in your current scene. Then, right-click -&gt; Duplicate or <code>ctrl/cmd + D</code>. The duplicate object will be disattached from the original Prefab. Change the name of the object, then drag/drop it in the project view to get a new Prefab.</p> <h3 id="learn-more"><a href="#learn-more" class="header-anchor">#</a> Learn More</h3> <p>To learn how you can use Prefabs in your components see the documentation for the <a href="/EngineAPI/Prefab">Prefab</a> class.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="app"><a href="#app" class="header-anchor">#</a> App</h3> <p>The <strong>App</strong> class is a singleton in charge of fetching and loading scenes when playing them from the editor or built project. Additionally, it gives us access to the current Scene and the active Camera.</p> <h3 id="properties"><a href="#properties" class="header-anchor">#</a> Properties</h3> <h4 id="activecamera"><a href="#activecamera" class="header-anchor">#</a> .activeCamera</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code>activeCamera<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This is the active camera rendering the current Scene.</p> <h4 id="title"><a href="#title" class="header-anchor">#</a> .title</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code>title<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This is the title of our App.</p> <h4 id="currentscene"><a href="#currentscene" class="header-anchor">#</a> .currentScene</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code>currentScene<span class="token operator">:</span> Scene<span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This is the currently active scene being rendered by the <strong>runtime</strong> controller.</p> <h4 id="scenes"><a href="#scenes" class="header-anchor">#</a> .scenes</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token keyword">readonly</span> scenes<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token punctuation">{</span> name<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">,</span> uuid<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">}</span><span class="token punctuation">[</span><span class="token punctuation">]</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This object contains a reference to the scenes that we wish to build with our app. The first one in the array will be run first.</p> <h3 id="methods"><a href="#methods" class="header-anchor">#</a> Methods</h3> <h4 id="play"><a href="#play" class="header-anchor">#</a> .play</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token function">play</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token keyword">void</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This function must be called to start the App only in the built project.</p> <h4 id="loadscene"><a href="#loadscene" class="header-anchor">#</a> .loadScene</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token function">loadScene</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span>name<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span> <span class="token operator">|</span> <span class="token builtin">number</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">Promise</span><span class="token operator">&lt;</span><span class="token keyword">void</span><span class="token operator">&gt;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>Call this function to asynchronously load a scene. Scenes, like all Assets in Rogue Engine, are not loaded in memory until after they are fetched in order to save on resources. The loaded scene will be immediately played by the <a href="/EngineAPI/Runtime">Runtime</a> controller.</p> <h4 id="clone"><a href="#clone" class="header-anchor">#</a> .clone</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token function">clone</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span>object3d<span class="token operator">:</span> Object3D<span class="token punctuation">,</span> parent<span class="token operator">?</span><span class="token operator">:</span> Object3D<span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">:</span> Object3D
</code></pre></div><p>This method clones a given <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/core/Object3D" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Object3D<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> with all its components. It's basically Rogue Engine's version of <strong>Object3D.clone</strong> which only clones the object.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="audioasset"><a href="#audioasset" class="header-anchor">#</a> AudioAsset</h3> <p>This class provides the necessary information to load a <strong>.rogueAudio</strong> file. An instance of this class aids us in creating either an <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/audio/Audio" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Audio<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> or <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/audio/PositionalAudio" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">PositionalAudio<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a> object.</p> <h2 id="properties"><a href="#properties" class="header-anchor">#</a> Properties</h2> <h4 id="uuid"><a href="#uuid" class="header-anchor">#</a> .uuid</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token keyword">readonly</span> uuid<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>The unique identifier of this asset.</p> <h4 id="path"><a href="#path" class="header-anchor">#</a> .path</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token keyword">readonly</span> path<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>The current path to the related <strong>.rogueAudio</strong> file.</p> <h4 id="name"><a href="#name" class="header-anchor">#</a> .name</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token keyword">readonly</span> name<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>The name of the asset.</p> <h2 id="methods"><a href="#methods" class="header-anchor">#</a> Methods</h2> <h4 id="getaudio"><a href="#getaudio" class="header-anchor">#</a> .getAudio</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token function">getAudio</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">:</span> Audio<span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This method creates an Audio object with the buffer information in the same instance of AudioAsset.</p> <h4 id="getpositionalaudio"><a href="#getpositionalaudio" class="header-anchor">#</a> .getPositionalAudio</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token function">getPositionalAudio</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">:</span> PositionalAudio<span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This method creates a PositionalAudio object with the buffer information in the same instance of AudioAsset.</p> <h4 id="audioasset-fromfile"><a href="#audioasset-fromfile" class="header-anchor">#</a> AudioAsset.fromFile</h4> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code><span class="token keyword">static</span> <span class="token function">fromFile</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span>filePath<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">Promise</span><span class="token operator">&lt;</span>AudioAsset<span class="token operator">&gt;</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>
</code></pre></div><p>This static method of AudioAsset takes the path of a <strong>.rogueAudio</strong> file and returns an instance of AudioAsset. You should use this method, instead of instantiating the class manually.</p></div>

<div class="theme-default-content content__default"><h3 id="component"><a href="#component" class="header-anchor">#</a> Component</h3> <p>A component adds functionality to an <a href="https://threejs.org/docs/#api/en/core/Object3D" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Object3D<span><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" x="0px" y="0px" viewBox="0 0 100 100" width="15" height="15" class="icon outbound"><path fill="currentColor" d="M18.8,85.1h56l0,0c2.2,0,4-1.8,4-4v-32h-8v28h-48v-48h28v-8h-32l0,0c-2.2,0-4,1.8-4,4v56C14.8,83.3,16.6,85.1,18.8,85.1z"></path> <polygon fill="currentColor" points="45.7,48.7 51.3,54.3 77.2,28.5 77.2,37.2 85.2,37.2 85.2,14.9 62.8,14.9 62.8,22.9 71.5,22.9"></polygon></svg> <span class="sr-only">(opens new window)</span></span></a>. An object can have multiple components but an instance of a component can be directly related to only one <strong>Object3D</strong>.</p> <p>The <strong>Component</strong> class defines the behavior of a component. We need to extend from this class to create our own components. We should never call the Component's methods ourselves. These are to be called exclusively within the Engine.</p> <p>When creating a component through the editor's interface (Assets -&gt; Component) a component will be created with the standard template, so you shouldn't have to create a component by hand but, in any case, let's see how hey are built.</p> <p>In the following example, we have a component <strong>MyComponent</strong> which extends the class <strong>Component</strong>. After defining it, we register it. If we don't register it, the component won't be accessible from anywhere. This is the way to let the Engine acknowledge it.</p> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code>
<span class="token keyword">import</span> <span class="token operator">*</span> <span class="token keyword">as</span> <span class="token constant">RE</span> <span class="token keyword">from</span> <span class="token string">'rogue-engine'</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>

<span class="token keyword">export</span> <span class="token keyword">class</span> <span class="token class-name">MyComponent</span> <span class="token keyword">extends</span> <span class="token class-name"><span class="token constant">RE</span></span><span class="token punctuation">.</span>Component <span class="token punctuation">{</span>
<span class="token operator">...</span>
<span class="token punctuation">}</span>

<span class="token constant">RE</span><span class="token punctuation">.</span><span class="token function">registerComponent</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span>MyComponent<span class="token punctuation">)</span><span class="token punctuation">;</span>

</code></pre></div><h3 id="constructor"><a href="#constructor" class="header-anchor">#</a> Constructor</h3> <div class="language-typescript extra-class"><pre class="language-typescript"><code>
<span class="token function">constructor</span><span class="token punctuation">(</span>name<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token builtin">string</span><span class="token punctuation">,</span> object3d<span class="token operator">:</span> <span class="token constant">THREE</span><span class="token punctuation">.</span>Object3D<span class="token punctuation">)</span>

</code></pre></div><p>The <strong>Component</strong> class constructor takes a <strong>name</strong> string and an <strong>Object3D</strong> as parameters. The <strong>Object3D</strong> will be assigned to the <strong>Component.object3d</strong> property.
